**出處**　3GPP TS 28.313 V18.2.0 clauses 6.4.2.4 and 8.3.4 (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/28_series/28.313/)

</details>

**089.** Express the tilt values used on 11-17, 11-19 and 11-21 in degrees and state where the 11-19 value lies relative to the policy range.

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　6.0° → 3.0° (11-17), 3.0° → 2.0° (11-19), 2.0° → 6.0° (11-21); the 11-19 value of 2.0° is exactly at the lower limit of the 2.0°–8.0° range.

**詳解**　digitalTilt is in 0.1-degree units: 60 = 6.0°, 30 = 3.0°, 20 = 2.0°; the range [20, 80] is 2.0° to 8.0°. The function could not raise the beam further without leaving the policy range, which is consistent with it giving up and restoring.

**出處**　3GPP TS 28.541 V18.15.0 clauses 4.3.74 and 4.4.1 (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/28_series/28.541/)

</details>

**090.** An engineer wants to set GE-0711-2's tilt manually to 1.5° to test coverage behind the building. Can you tell from the record whether the C-SON function would later override such a manual change?

1. Yes, it would never override, because C-SON only acts on SON-made values
2. Cannot determine manual-override behavior; 1.5° is outside the configured tilt range.
3. Yes, it would always override immediately, because cCOControl = TRUE
4. Yes, the manual value would be accepted, because manual changes have priority over SON by specification

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　2

**詳解**　The export shows CCO actions but nothing about conflict handling with manual changes; TS 28.313 only lets an authorised consumer enable/disable CCO via cCOControl (CCO switch) and defines the policy ranges. 15 is below the range minimum 20. Options 1, 3 and 4 state behaviours that are not in the data or in the cited specifications.

**出處**　3GPP TS 28.313 V18.2.0 Table 7.2.3.2.2-1 (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/28_series/28.313/); 3GPP TS 28.541 V18.15.0 clause 4.3.70 (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/28_series/28.541/)

</details>

#### Telling coverage apart from capacity, interference and uplink limits in mixed evidence

**情境 019**　Complaints around a stadium and a riverside path

The ticket system of NorthLink Mobile (fictional) mapped six complaints to cell AR-0450-1. The stadium hosted matches on 11-15 and 11-22 in the evening.

```
Tickets mapped to AR-0450-1, 2026-11-12 .. 11-25
ticket  date   time   place               customer text
T-01    11-15  20:10  stadium gate        "full bars, nothing loads"
T-02    11-15  20:40  stadium stands      "full bars, videos freeze"
T-03    11-18  13:05  riverside path      "no signal at all"
T-04    11-22  19:55  stadium stands      "very slow"
T-05    11-22  21:15  stadium gate        "can't send photos"
T-06    11-20  08:30  riverside path      "No service under the bridge"

AR-0450-1, 20:00-21:00          match days (11-15, 11-22)   other days (average)
RRU.PrbDl (%)                    97                          35
RRC.ConnMax                      1,850                       420
SS-SINR<0dB share                 9%                          8%
SS-RSRP<-115dBm share             4%                          4%
```

**091.** How should the stadium complaints (T-01, T-02, T-04, T-05) be classified first?

1. Capacity issue: complaints coincide with matches and higher load.
2. As weak coverage, because customers say nothing loads
3. As overlapping coverage, because many UEs are in one place
4. As overshoot, because users come from far away to the stadium

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　1

**詳解**　The complaints are tied to event hours, "full bars" matches the unchanged RSRP/SINR shares (4%/4% and 9%/8%), while RRU.PrbDl rises from 35% to 97% and RRC.ConnMax from 420 to 1,850: the capacity signature. Option 2 is contradicted by the unchanged RSRP distribution and "full bars". Option 3 confuses many UEs with many comparable cells. Option 4 confuses where users come from with where the radio signal reaches.

**出處**　3GPP TS 28.552 V18.12.0 clauses 5.1.1.2.1 and 5.1.1.4.2 (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/28_series/28.552/); Curriculum 6.1.1 (distinguishing coverage from capacity)

</details>

**092.** What distinguishes the riverside complaints (T-03, T-06)?

1. They are part of the same capacity problem, because they were mapped to the same cell
2. They prove an RU fault, because "no signal" means the cell was off
3. Same-place, non-event complaints of no service suggest a separate coverage issue.
4. They are interference complaints, because SINR shares are low in the cell

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　3

**詳解**　Coverage problems follow the location, not the time: T-03 (13:05) and T-06 (08:30) are on ordinary days at the same place, unrelated to load. Option 1 ignores time and place. Option 2 is wrong because complaints from a single place do not prove the cell was off; nothing in the data shows an outage, and the problem is confined to one location. Option 4 misreads the cell-level SINR shares, which are unchanged and say nothing about the bridge.

**出處**　Curriculum 6.1.1 (coverage follows location, not time); 3GPP TS 37.320 V18.5.0 Annex A (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/37_series/37.320/)

</details>

**093.** Which pattern over time and place points to a coverage problem rather than a capacity problem?

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　A coverage problem is tied to a location and appears there regardless of the hour or load, whereas a capacity problem appears in busy periods with high PRB usage while the RSRP/SINR distributions stay unchanged.

**詳解**　The textbook rule: low RSRP and low SINR point to coverage; unchanged RSRP, SINR and CQI distributions with high busy-hour PRB usage and falling throughput point to capacity (6.1.4); coverage problems usually follow "where", not "when".

**出處**　Curriculum 6.1.1 (distinguishing from interference and capacity); 3GPP TS 28.552 V18.12.0 clauses 5.1.1.2.1 and 5.1.1.3.1 (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/28_series/28.552/)

</details>

**094.** What does the TS 28.552 measurement RRU.PrbDl represent?

1. The mean number of PRBs used in the downlink for data traffic, on NRCellCU
2. RRU.PrbDl is DL PRB usage (%) per NRCellDU, integer 0–100.
3. The distribution of DL PRB usage samples in vendor-specific bins
4. The total number of PRBs available in the downlink

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　2

**詳解**　TS 28.552 clause 5.1.1.2.1 (DL Total PRB Usage) defines RRU.PrbDl as the percentage of downlink PRBs used for any purpose, a single integer from 0 to 100, measured object NRCellDU. Option 1 describes RRU.PrbUsedDl (clause 5.1.1.2.5, a PRB count, and with the wrong object). Option 3 describes RRU.PrbTotDlDist.Bin (clause 5.1.1.2.3). Option 4 describes RRU.PrbAvailDl (clause 5.1.1.2.6).

**出處**　3GPP TS 28.552 V18.12.0 clauses 5.1.1.2.1, 5.1.1.2.3, 5.1.1.2.5 and 5.1.1.2.6 (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/28_series/28.552/)

</details>

**095.** Is the bridge on the riverside path inside the area that AR-0450-1 is planned to serve?

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　Cannot be determined: the tickets were mapped to AR-0450-1 by the ticket system only; the radio plan (planned service areas and best-server assignment for the bridge location) is needed.

**詳解**　Whether the issue is "AR-0450-1 does not reach its planned area" or "the bridge was never planned to be covered by this cell" changes the owner and the fix. Neither the tickets nor the cell KPIs contain planning data.

**出處**　Curriculum 6.1.1 (planned coverage versus actual); 3GPP TS 37.320 V18.5.0 Annex A (coverage mapping, cell boundary mapping) (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/37_series/37.320/)

</details>

**情境 020**　Per-beam statistics of one cell

Telco-X (fictional) analysed one week of per-SSB statistics for cell YL-0233-2 (eight SSB beams). "Neighbours within 6 dB" was derived from neighbour results in the same measurement reports (fictional operator analysis).

```
Cell YL-0233-2, 2026-11-17 .. 11-23, busy hour
Sources: L1M.SS-RSRP.Bin (per Beam), MR.SS-SINRPerSSB.Bin (per Beam), neighbour results in MRs

SSB  median SS-RSRP (dBm)  median SS-SINR (dB)  median neighbours within 6 dB
0    -92                   14                   0
1    -95                   12                   1
2    -90                   -2                   0
3    -91                   -3                   0
4    -96                   11                   1
5    -98                    9                   1
6    -93                   -1                   3
7    -118                   0                   0
RRU.PrbDl (cell): 28%
```

**096.** How should beams 2 and 3 be classified?

1. Weak coverage, because SINR is negative
2. Overlapping coverage, because SINR is negative while RSRP is good
3. Capacity shortage, because SINR collapses in busy hour
4. Good RSRP, low SINR and no strong own neighbour: external interference.

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　4

**詳解**　RSRP -90/-91 dBm is good; low SINR with good RSRP is the interference family; with no own cell within 6 dB, the interference source is not an identifiable own cell, so overlapping coverage is excluded and 6.1.2 applies. Option 1 ignores the good RSRP. Option 2 ignores the zero neighbour count. Option 3 is contradicted by 28% PRB usage.

**出處**　3GPP TS 38.215 V18.4.0 clauses 5.1.1 and 5.1.5 (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/38_series/38.215/); Curriculum 6.1.1

</details>

**097.** Which beam shows the overlapping-coverage signature?

1. Beam 0
2. Beam 7
3. Beam 6
4. Beam 5

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　3

**詳解**　Beam 6 has good RSRP (-93 dBm), negative SINR (-1 dB) and a median of three neighbours within 6 dB: several comparable cells, none dominant. Option 1 is a healthy beam (14 dB SINR, no comparable neighbours). Option 2 has weak RSRP (-118 dBm), i.e. weak coverage. Option 4 has one comparable neighbour and 9 dB SINR, which is acceptable.

**出處**　Curriculum 6.1.1 (characteristics table); 3GPP TS 37.320 V18.5.0 Annex A (pilot pollution) (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/37_series/37.320/)

</details>

**098.** How should beam 7 be classified, and why?

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　Weak coverage: RSRP is low (-118 dBm) and SINR is low (0 dB) with no comparable neighbour, so the wanted signal itself is weak and noise dominates.

**詳解**　Low RSRP plus low SINR is the weak-coverage signature, and with zero neighbours within 6 dB there is no interfering own cell explaining the SINR. Possible causes (obstruction, beam direction, tilt) need further checks.

**出處**　3GPP TS 38.215 V18.4.0 clauses 5.1.1 and 5.1.5 (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/38_series/38.215/); Curriculum 6.1.1

</details>

**099.** Which TS 28.552 measurement provides the distribution of the serving cell's SS-SINR per SSB beam, and on which object?

1. MR.SS-SINRPerSSB.Bin, measured object Beam
2. MR.NRScSSSINR.BinX, measured object Beam
3. MR.SS-SINR.SSBIndex.Bin, measured object NRCellCU
4. L1M.SS-RSRP.Bin, measured object NRCellRelation

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　1

**詳解**　TS 28.552 clause 5.1.1.32.2 defines MR.SS-SINRPerSSB.Bin (SS-SINR distribution per SSB of the serving cell) with object Beam. Option 2 is the cell-level distribution (clause 5.1.1.32.1), whose object is NRCellCU. Option 3 is the per-SSB distribution of a neighbour cell (clause 5.1.1.32.3), on NRCellRelation. Option 4 is an RSRP measurement whose object is Beam.

**出處**　3GPP TS 28.552 V18.12.0 clauses 5.1.1.22.1, 5.1.1.32.1, 5.1.1.32.2 and 5.1.1.32.3 (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/28_series/28.552/)

</details>

**100.** For beams 2 and 3, can you tell from this table whether the cause is an external interferer or a problem with own cells that UEs do not report (e.g. undetected or PCI-related)?

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　Cannot be determined: the table only contains downlink statistics of reported cells; the PCI plan of surrounding (including distant) cells and interference evidence in the beams' direction (e.g. interference counters or a spectrum scan) are needed, which belong to the 6.1.2 checks.

**詳解**　"No neighbour within 6 dB" only means no reported own cell is comparable. Whether the energy comes from an outside source or from an own cell that is not reported needs other data, which the coverage analysis cannot supply.

**出處**　Curriculum 6.1.1 (hand-over to 6.1.2 when no matching cell is found); 3GPP TS 28.552 V18.12.0 clauses 5.1.1.22.2 and 5.1.1.32.2 (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/28_series/28.552/)

</details>

**情境 021**　Highway sites and the mid-point between them

Telco-X (fictional) reviews a rural highway with n78 sites (SCS 30 kHz). Mid-point values come from Immediate MDT M1 and M2 records tagged with location; the design rule is a fictional operator rule.

```
Highway HW, week 2026-11-16 .. 11-22
site pair    inter-site distance  mid-point median SS-RSRP (M1)  mid-point median power headroom (M2)  RLF reports in mid-point zone
HW-01/HW-02  1.6 km               -104 dBm                       +12 dB                                3
HW-02/HW-03  2.9 km               -117 dBm                        -2 dB                                41
HW-03/HW-04  1.7 km               -106 dBm                        +9 dB                                5

Design rule (fictional operator rule): maximum inter-site distance on n78 highway sections = 2.0 km
```

**101.** Which statement fits the data?

1. All three pairs comply with the design rule and the RLF difference is random
2. HW-02/HW-03 exceeds 2.0 km and has weak midpoint DL/UL plus more RLFs.
3. HW-01/HW-02 is the problem pair, because it has the best power headroom
4. HW-03/HW-04 exceeds the rule, because 1.7 km is close to 2.0 km

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　2

**詳解**　2.9 − 2.0 = 0.9 km over the rule; at the mid-point both DL (RSRP -117 dBm) and UL (negative power headroom, the UE has no power to spare) are weak, and RLF reports are 41 versus 3 and 5. Option 1 ignores the 2.9 km pair. Option 3 reads good headroom as a problem. Option 4 is wrong because 1.7 km is within the rule.

**出處**　3GPP TS 37.320 V18.5.0 clause 5.4.1.1 (M1, M2) (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/37_series/37.320/); design rule is a fictional operator rule

</details>

**102.** What does TS 37.320 Annex A say about uplink (UL) coverage?

1. UL coverage problems are always caused by external interference
2. UL coverage is only relevant for E-UTRA, not NR
3. UL coverage is outside the scope of MDT
4. Poor UL can cause setup failure, drops or poor voice; balance UL and DL coverage.

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　4

**詳解**　Annex A lists "UL coverage": poor UL coverage might impact call setup failure, call drop and poor UL voice quality, coverage should be balanced between UL and DL, and optimisation can adapt site configuration (antennas) and UL-related parameters. Options 1–3 contradict or are absent from that text.

**出處**　3GPP TS 37.320 V18.5.0 Annex A (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/37_series/37.320/)

</details>

**103.** For the HW-02/HW-03 pair, how far is the mid-point from each site, and roughly which TA index does that correspond to at SCS 30 kHz?

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　About 1,450 m from each site, i.e. a TA index of about 37.

**詳解**　2.9 km / 2 = 1,450 m. One TA step at 30 kHz ≈ 39.0625 m (512 Tc round trip, halved, c ≈ 3×10^8 m/s). 1,450 / 39.0625 ≈ 37.1, so about index 37 (straight-line geometry assumed; reflections can make TA larger).

**出處**　3GPP TS 38.213 V18.9.0 clause 4.2 (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/38_series/38.213/); 3GPP TS 38.215 V18.4.0 clause 5.2.7 (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/38_series/38.215/)

</details>

**104.** Which site pair exceeds the operator's maximum spacing, and what evidence makes it the highest-priority follow-up?

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　HW-02/HW-03; its 2.9 km spacing exceeds 2.0 km, and it also has the weakest M1, negative M2 and most RLF reports.

**詳解**　The fictional design rule sets a 2.0 km maximum. HW-02/HW-03 is 2.9 km and has −117 dBm M1, −2 dB M2 and 41 RLF reports, all more concerning than the other listed pairs.

**出處**　3GPP TS 37.320 V18.5.0 clause 5.4.1.1 (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/37_series/37.320/)

</details>

**105.** Do the HW-02/HW-03 RLFs happen because the downlink fails first or because the uplink fails first?

1. The downlink fails first, because -117 dBm is below the operator's report threshold
2. The uplink fails first, because the power headroom is negative
3. Cannot determine; UL quality and RLF cause details are missing.
4. Neither, because the RLFs are caused by the large inter-site distance and not by radio conditions

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　3

**詳解**　Weak RSRP and exhausted power headroom are both present, so either link could break first; the table contains no UL quality measurement or failure cause. Option 1 and option 2 each pick one indicator and ignore the other. Option 4 is wrong because the distance acts through the radio conditions; it is not a separate cause.

**出處**　3GPP TS 37.320 V18.5.0 clauses 5.4.1.1 and 5.4.1.2, Annex A (UL coverage) (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/37_series/37.320/); Curriculum 6.1.1 and 6.1.10

</details>

### GSMA 練習題（171 題）

**1.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**17.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.

**29.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**38.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**42.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**46.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**50.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**54.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**58.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**67.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**71.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**75.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**84.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**93.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**102.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**107.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.
